Rationale: The driver did what it could and said so (JC-358). The coordinating session then sent the cause to review 5, which ruled it a defect against the owner's SPEC.md, and wrote the retest into test F's purpose: "whether a delegated round still stops at a `question` (fix 5, R5-M1)".
Spec conflict: The owner's SPEC.md line 13 is the sentence review 5 used to decide this was a defect rather than a test design error; the harness's own rules document contradicted itself on the point, which is what let the ambiguity survive to a real round.
Other context: Test F then raised the question in a delegated round and resumed it with an answer, so the missing half was covered without a second test D. One part remains untested by a real agent: an override issued at a question checkpoint, which is pinned only by the fixer's unit tests and the reviewer's reproductions.
Sources: bootstrap/tests/test-D.md section 1 and section 8.1; bootstrap/reviews/review-5.md R5-M1; bootstrap/fixes/fix-5.md; bootstrap/tests/test-F.md; bootstrap/FINAL-REPORT.md section 3.

## JC-473: Test E built from probe repositories instead of a sandbox round (from JC-COORD-37)
Who and where: the second coordinating session, bootstrap/tests/test-E.md and commit 037a6cb.
Context: The handoff asked whether the quality gates actually catch planted defects, and then whether the retry path works. A full sandbox round shows one gate judging one artifact; the session had to decide how to see all six gates judge both a clean and a deliberately broken artifact, and where the retry would be walked.
Rationale: Recorded in the report's opening: "Level 1 of `harness/docs/TESTING.md`: the six LLM gates run by real sub-agents on a clean and a planted artifact through `probe` and scored with `probe --check` (part 1), then the FAIL -> producer -> gate retry walked inside two probe repositories with `record` and `next` (part 2)." Twelve cells, two retries, sixteen sub-agents in all.
Spec conflict: The owner's SPEC.md names this shape as one of the architecture features: "A stub agent with many scripted behaviors drives every path; real agents run as probes with planted defects." The owner's file does not say how many cells, which gates, or that the retry should be walked inside a probe repository rather than a round.
Other context: The choice worked and was cheaper than rounds: sixteen sub-agents, about 25 minutes, and 12 of 12 cells as expected - the evidence all six planners had said they would wait for before touching the gate prose (JC-77, JC-84). It also exposed something undocumented, which the report flags: "that a probe repository is a live round you can drive on with `next` and `record` (all of part 2) is documented nowhere."
Sources: bootstrap/tests/test-E.md; commit 037a6cb; SPEC.md; harness/docs/TESTING.md.

## JC-474: Test E judged by one model in every cell (from JC-COORD-38)
Who and where: the second coordinating session, in the brief as test E's driver reports it.
Context: The harness prints a rung for each gate, and for these gates the printed rung is the top one. The driver could not run that model (JC-454). The session had to choose between letting each cell run on whatever was available and fixing one model for all twelve.
Rationale: Quoted: "DRIVER.md 30 says `model` = `model_alias`. My brief fixed the model at `opus` so that one model judges all twelve cells and both retries, which is what makes the matrix comparable. Consequence: every run is off the printed rung, and the effort was my session's, not the action's `max`."
Spec conflict: The owner's harness/subAgents.yaml assigns each rung its own model and price, and SPEC.md records why the printed rung could not be honoured. Nothing in the owner's files says what a tester should do about it.
Other context: The result is that gate effectiveness is proved for one model only, and the final report says so rather than claiming more: "every clean seed PASSed, every planted defect FAILed with the planted text quoted, on Opus gates; the printed probe rung is `max`, which the drivers could not run." The report also notes a gap the choice revealed: a probe has no way to be told which rung actually ran, so nothing flags the substitution - which is the driver's own headline caveat (JC-425).
Sources: bootstrap/tests/test-E.md section 6; bootstrap/FINAL-REPORT.md section 3; harness/subAgents.yaml; SPEC.md.

## JC-475: Spawn and time budgets set for each driver (from JC-COORD-39)
Who and where: the second coordinating session, visible in what the drivers report about their briefs.
Context: A test driver spawns paid agents in a loop and can, in principle, keep retrying. The session had to decide whether to cap that, and it also had to decide how much observation to ask for inside the loop: test F's brief asked for a status reading after every attempt.
Rationale: Not recorded beyond the numbers. Expected rationale (the auditor's inference): a hard ceiling on spawns and wall clock is the cheapest protection against a driver that loops on a broken path, and it lets the coordinator plan the remaining work; the observation request exists because test F's whole subject was what the harness reports, so more readings meant more evidence.
Spec conflict: None found; the owner's files say nothing about test budgets.
Other context: Neither cap bound: test E used 16 of 30 spawns in about 25 minutes of a five-hour allowance, and test F used 8 of 25. The observation request was not met in full, and the driver said so rather than claiming coverage (JC-440). A related instruction is recorded in test E, that the driver should not force the failure limit.
Sources: bootstrap/tests/test-E.md section 7 and section 6; bootstrap/tests/test-F.md section 1 and section 8.

## JC-476: Test F designed as one round with two deliberate gaps and seven surfaces to check (from JC-COORD-40)
Who and where: the second coordinating session, bootstrap/tests/test-F.md and commit 0851d49.
Context: The handoff's last test had to "fabricate a situation that forces an undefined judgment call... and check that the harness records it... and surfaces it at `record`, at checkpoints, in `status` and at `done`". The session had to design a situation that forces one, decide how many places counted as "surfaced", and decide whether to reuse the round to retest the fix from JC-472.
Rationale: Recorded in the report: "one delegated sandbox round built to force exactly one undefined judgment call the owner refused to be asked about (gap A) and exactly one question the owner reserved for themselves (gap B). The point of the pass is the **reporting**... Gap A was picked because it is a genuine behaviour fork with no mention anywhere in the prose, and gap B because it is the function's headline behaviour with exactly two sensible answers, one line of code either way."
Spec conflict: The situation was built directly against the owner's harness/AGENTS.md: "A blanket ask such as 'do what is sensible' defines how you choose, not the line between the two: when the spec, plan, code, tests, or docs conflict or fall silent on something that impacts behavior, the judgment call is UNDEFINED even though the prose told you to be sensible." The owner's file defines the rule; nothing in it says how to test that the machinery obeys it, or how many places must show it.
Other context: The session widened the handoff's four surfaces to seven, adding the round history, the two judgment-call files, and the owner's answer reaching the landed code, and all seven matched. The round also carried the retest of the restored question pause, so one round did two jobs. A by-product the report is honest about: the checkpoint dutifully showed the owner the very decision they had said they did not want to be asked about, which was filed as a question for the owner rather than a defect (JC-441). The choice of the two gaps themselves is JC-426 and JC-427.
Sources: bootstrap/tests/test-F.md; commit 0851d49; harness/AGENTS.md; git show ca24820:bootstrap/HANDOFF.md step 7.

## JC-477: Reviews 4 and 5 told to grade any defect on the next test's path as major at least (from JC-COORD-41)
Who and where: the second coordinating session, in the briefs as the reviewers quote them.
Context: A reviewer's severity scale decides what gets fixed. Because each review sat immediately before a paid test, the session had to decide whether findings on the imminent path should be graded on their own merits or promoted.
Rationale: Recorded as the instruction the reviewers quote: "A defect on one of the three upcoming paths is a major at least, as the assignment says", and for review 5, "A defect on test E's or the final test's path is a major at least". Expected rationale (the auditor's inference): a minor defect on a path that is about to be walked by real agents costs a whole round, which is not minor; promoting them guarantees the fixer clears the path before the money is spent. Review 5's own process gives the sharper version for its case: "No review follows test E, so anything on this path is a major at least" (JC-459).
Spec conflict: None found; the owner's files do not govern severity.
Other context: It changed what was built. Review 5's four majors were all fixed the same day, before tests E and F ran, and the two tests then confirmed several of them in passing: test F confirmed the restored question pause (JC-373), and test E deliberately provoked two of fix 5's guard refusals (JC-423) and scored its matrix with the corrected probe scoring (JC-377). The rule pairs with the brief's other half, that the reviewer must give a verdict on each item raised by the preceding test report, which is how a driver's complaint became either a fix or a recorded refusal.
Sources: bootstrap/reviews/review-4.md section 1; bootstrap/reviews/review-5.md section 1; bootstrap/fixes/fix-5.md; bootstrap/FINAL-REPORT.md section 4.

## JC-478: Four recurring complaints declared the owner's to settle rather than defects (from JC-COORD-42)
Who and where: the second coordinating session, bootstrap/FINAL-REPORT.md section 6, resting on the verdicts in reviews 4 and 5 and on the fix reports.
Context: The real rounds kept producing complaints that were not obviously bugs: whether archiving a test file should earn a refund, what an instruction about expected rejections means when every gate is off, how "one unit test" squares with "exhaust edge cases, within reason", and whether a step's budget should be carved out of a quote that also pays for other things. Each had to be classified as a harness defect to fix or a question only the owner can answer.
Rationale: Recorded as a heading and a sentence: "Questions the rounds raised for the owner. These reached CLARIFICATIONS.md in the sandbox rounds and were judged the owner's to settle, not harness defects." The individual verdicts are in the reviews and in the fix reports' item lists, for example "The ledger paying to archive a test file (test B): no harness change; the owner's numbers... a `living_charge` exemption is the owner's to decide" (JC-260, JC-267, JC-315).
Spec conflict: Three of the four are conflicts inside the owner's own locked prose and numbers, which is why they could not be settled by an agent: the phrases quoted are from PLAN-AGENTS-OVERVIEW.txt and PLAN-TO-SPEC-OVERVIEW.txt, and the fourth is about the owner's cost settings. The sessions' own standing rule is what forced the classification: "Nothing mechanical may depend on the wording of the locked prose."
Other context: The classification is what keeps the harness from encoding an answer the owner never gave, and it is the reason the final report ends with a list of four open questions rather than four silent design decisions. The alternative, having a fixer choose, was available and was rejected each time. The same four questions reached the harness's own clarifications file through the postmortems of the rounds that met them (JC-242, JC-245, JC-246, JC-301).
Sources: bootstrap/FINAL-REPORT.md sections 5 and 6; bootstrap/tests/test-C.md section 1; test-B.md section 5; bootstrap/reviews/review-4.md section 1; harness/locked_prose/PLAN-AGENTS-OVERVIEW.txt; harness/locked_prose/PLAN-TO-SPEC-OVERVIEW.txt; harness/project.yaml; git show ca24820:bootstrap/HANDOFF.md.

## JC-479: The final report went beyond the list it was given (from JC-COORD-43)
Who and where: the second coordinating session, bootstrap/FINAL-REPORT.md and commit 6cbfc97.
Context: The handoff specified seven things the report must contain (JC-467). The writing session had to decide what else the owner should be told, including things that reflect on the sessions themselves.
Rationale: Not stated as a reason; the choices are visible in the file. It adds a table of all six tests with their configuration, outcome, token totals and ledger spend; a table of the review and fix cycle with the counts of findings at each stage; the four open questions (JC-478); the cost of its own coordination, agent by agent, "Total about 3.58 million, over about nine hours of wall clock"; and a self-reported bookkeeping slip: "R5-n4 was fixed but its DRIVER.md sentence landed in the R5-M1 commit, whose subject omits the id."
Spec conflict: None found; the owner's files impose no reporting duty beyond spec.yaml's "flag every edit loudly", which the report's first section satisfies.
Other context: The coordination auditor checked the self-reported slip and it is true: the commit whose subject names only two findings does change the driver document. Disclosing the coordination's own token cost and a mislabelled commit was a choice; neither was asked for, and neither flatters the work.
Sources: bootstrap/FINAL-REPORT.md sections 3, 4, 5, 6 and 8; commits 6cbfc97 and 3f613d7; git show ca24820:bootstrap/HANDOFF.md step 8.

## JC-480: The handoff rewritten in place and marked complete (from JC-COORD-44)
Who and where: the second coordinating session, commit 6cbfc97 and bootstrap/HANDOFF.md as it now stands.
Context: The file that had carried the instructions for this session was finished with. The session had to decide whether to leave it as a record, delete it, or replace its contents with a closing note; and it had to decide which of the old standing rules still applied to anyone who works here later.
Rationale: The new text states the position: "The eight remaining steps of the previous handoff are done... Nothing is pending... What follows is the owner's: Level 3 of `harness/docs/TESTING.md`", followed by a five-clause restatement of the rules "that still apply to any later agent session here".
Spec conflict: None found; the owner's files do not govern this file.
Other context: The replacement is total, so the first session's handoff, including its ordered plan and its known-defect diagnosis, now exists only inside the git history, which is why several entries in this audit had to read it with `git show ca24820:bootstrap/HANDOFF.md`. The new rule list keeps five rules - the owner's files, prose independence, one worktree-synced sub-agent at a time, the push policy, sandbox-only rounds - and drops three - the model-by-role assignment (JC-462), the no-nested-spawning rule with its driver exception (JC-461), and where reports go - so a later session reading only this file would not know them.
Sources: commit 6cbfc97; bootstrap/HANDOFF.md (current); git show ca24820:bootstrap/HANDOFF.md; bootstrap/FINAL-REPORT.md.
